### Completed issue #3 — merged counterexample (2026-11-20)

AgentDrift is a **third-party synthetic dataset**, not authentic production telemetry. The isolated two-record adaptation shows one correctly allowed benign trace and one expected unauthorized trace allowed by a deliberately permissive tool-name policy. PR #4 merged to `main` at commit `3cf052ad2c77f804cf945c4e41ac674c73a76cf1`, with GitHub Actions run `44678337210` 4/4 PASS. Re-running tests on merged KIRAA source: **31/31 PASS**; GitHub issue #3 CLOSED. This completes the *narrow external counterexample* subtask ATL-004A; the broader external/real-world evaluation task ATL-004 is still IN_PROGRESS.

Released version **v0.2.0** remains available at `https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/releases/tag/v0.2.0` with verified Python wheel (SHA256 `dd2382604b68c609be4bdd0edf84cf3239ecb0bc4638b0bebfd24f6062ed4e1a`). The post-release case-study commit is on `main` but is not part of the tagged v0.2.0 wheel.

**NEXT_ACTION:** [Issue #5](https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/issues/5), ATL-009 task 1 — design explicit **user-task authorization inputs**, including a legitimate email-forward case, an unauthorized forwarding case, and deny-by-default behavior when user-grant evidence is missing. Use Cedar and OPA's documented authorization concepts as references, not as dependencies. Do not let an LLM guess permissions; require externally supplied authorization facts. Then implement in a separate small task and verify compatibility against the 31-test baseline. Continue hunting for consented authentic tool traces; do not call the two AgentDrift samples a statistically representative benchmark.

### ATL-004BF OTLP numeric AnyValue fail-closed defect — 2026-11-21

- RECOVER/OWNERSHIP: KIRAA / KPC Core, existing PUBLIC kira-agent-trace-lab source, no project.state/KPC registration. Pre-work main 1d00f0c clean/synced; no conflicting open PRs. No real customer traces were imported or executed.
- VERIFIED_ROOT_CAUSE: Pre-fix _otlp_value({'doubleValue': 10**350}, 'safe') raised UNHANDLED Python OverflowError in math.isfinite(int) instead of SuiteError/CLI safe exit; _otlp_value({'intValue': str(2**64)}, 'safe') INCORRECTLY returned an out-of-range integer. These were reproduced on actual source before editing, not assumed. The OTLP AnyValue intValue wire type is signed int64. Exposes a malformed-input reliability failure, NOT a verified real-world exploit or policy bypass.
- IMPLEMENTATION: One small coherent fix to agent_trace_lab/otlp.py: parse intValue and require -2^63 <= integer < 2^63; convert numeric doubleValue via guarded float(...) and reject out-of-range/non-finite values using redacted location-only SuiteError, never an unhandled numeric OverflowError. Existing supported boundary values and finite double behavior preserved. Added three focused regression test methods to tests/test_otlp.py: min/max signed int64, huge integer double/NaN/inf rejection and actual CLI input file safe exit=2 with no traceback or output artifact. No release-gate, task-grant, import/export label, or runtime instrumentation changes.

### ATL-004BG verified OTLP execute-tool span identity and duplicate replay rejection — 2026-11-21

- RECOVERY/OWNER: KIRAA / KPC Core. Existing public `C:\Projects\kira-agent-trace-lab` initial clean/synced main 3164378550af7cf29ff9a5d2bd8570cd0360a76a; no new `project.state.json`/KPC registration or conflicting open PR. No change to active KPC/KiraShop sessions.
- PROVEN ROOT CAUSE: Existing `import_otlp` recorded any `execute_tool` span with a traceId/time but never validated `spanId`, permitting replay of a copied span to count as a second tool call and permitting missing or all-zero span identities. Reproduced **pre-edit** with exact copied synthetic `execute_tool` span: importer returned three actions instead of two; also accepted missing/zero `spanId`. Added regression tests first: **eight expected test failures** against old importer, including CLI exit 0 on replay rather than expected 2. Authoritative https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/blob/main/opentelemetry/proto/trace/v1/trace.proto and https://opentelemetry.io/docs/specs/otlp/ require a valid nonzero 16-byte trace ID, nonzero 8-byte unique span ID, case-insensitive hex encoding in OTLP/JSON.
- DELIVERED/REVIEW: Scoped four-file change: validate nonzero hex `traceId` and mandatory nonzero 16-hex `spanId` on recorded execute-tool spans; reject duplicates case-insensitively across scope groups instead of deduplicating or double counting; update original self-authored synthetic fixture to include distinct hex span IDs; extend `tests/test_otlp.py` with direct duplicate/zero/missing/invalid/casing coverage, cross-scope and actual CLI failure without private argument echo or output artifact; document strict recorded-span contract and excluded unrelated span validation in README. No execution of recorded tools, no new inferred human-grant labels, no policy change, no imported personal traces. This is importer data integrity, NOT proven real-world security exploit prevention.
- EVIDENCE: KIRAA local targeted OTLP **25/25 PASS** and full **369 tests: 365 PASS / four existing platform privilege SKIPs** before and after merge. Actual diff reviewed, git diff --check PASS. Fix commit 6bfd739ffb7e12540a3e38f9f89cd60abef14cbf. ### ATL-004BH verified OTLP fixed64 timestamp bounds and numeric JSON support — 2026-11-21

- RECOVERY/ROUTE: Verified KIRAA/KPC Core, existing public agent-trace-lab clean/synced main fc94cf31def636e7000867d710e25709a458f7d0 at task start, previous [main CI 44854867653](https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/actions/runs/44854867653) SUCCESS. - PROVEN DEFECT: OTLP span start_time_unix_nano is protobuf `fixed64` (unsigned 64-bit); OTLP JSON decoding permits a decimal string OR numeric JSON value: https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/blob/main/opentelemetry/proto/trace/v1/trace.proto and https://github.com/open-telemetry/opentelemetry-proto/blob/main/docs/specification.md . Before edits, KIRAA `import_otlp` REJECTED valid integer timestamp 1795146000000000000, ACCEPTED out-of-range decimal string `2**64` as a timestamp, and accepted non-ASCII Unicode digits. Regression tests were added before code change and failed with 3 failures / four errors as expected; actual CLI erroneously returned success for over-range timestamp.
- IMPLEMENTATION: Minimal `agent_trace_lab/otlp.py` change: accept JSON Python int or strict 1–20 ASCII-digit string, normalize to integer, require 0 <= time < 2**64; reject booleans/floats, negative/oversized values and non-ASCII digits with location-only SuiteError; use validated integer for stable temporal ordering. Extended `tests/test_otlp.py` with boundary, invalid-input privacy, positive/negative end-to-end CLI tests. Updated README timestamp contract. No invented labels, saved real traces, altered authorization policy, new dependencies or release.
- EXACT_TEST_EVIDENCE: Source fix commit 404080dd8f06c05ec9fdf2f55bb07ee117a0e704, three reviewed files, staged diff --check PASS. Local focused **29/29 OTLP PASS**; full suite **373 tests: 369 PASS, four existing Windows privilege SKIPs**, same after merge on KIRAA. ### ATL-004BN official Inspect approval-versus-execution evidence gate — 2026-11-21

- ROUTE/CHECKPOINT: KIRAA, KPC Core, existing agent-trace-lab clean/synced main 79a4d4ba6d838bf94e3107a249321471cbf99968 before task; no project.state/KPC registration, conflicting PR, or private source data accessed. Exact prior docs CI for 4fbb08b was SUCCESS at [44857830381](https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/actions/runs/44857830381).
- SOURCE CONTRACT: Current official [Inspect approval](https://inspect.aisi.org.uk/approval.html) states that bridged agent tool calls are approved before handout but not executed by Inspect; an approved call can fail to run after another tool in the same response is rejected; automatic approval records can be absent. [Inspect event API](https://inspect.aisi.org.uk/reference/inspect_ai.event.html) has ApprovalEvent fields event, call, execution_id, approver, decision, modified and ToolEvent fields event, id, execution_id, function, arguments, result, error. The opaque execution_id is experimental, optional, absent in legacy logs; for a bridge it can denote the handout, not a completed tool execution. [OTel GenAI execute_tool conventions](https://github.com/open-telemetry/semantic-conventions-genai/blob/main/docs/gen-ai/execute-tool.md) mark tool arguments/results OPT-IN. [Inspect license](https://github.com/UKGovernmentBEIS/inspect_ai/blob/main/LICENSE) is MIT, which does NOT confer rights to private real eval logs. These are documentation facts, not tested local Inspect runtime exports.
- ACTUAL SYNTHETIC PROBE: On KIRAA ran current engine.evaluate_suite() with fictional guarded demo.tool and argument synthetic=true. Approval-only/no trace: **0 events, allow** (no supplied actions). Naive fictional importer converts approval into both fabricated trace call and grant: **1 event, allow**. Recorded call with no grant: **1 event, deny/TASK_GRANT_REQUIRED**. The second case is a hypothetical importer fabrication risk, **NOT a discovered vulnerability or permission bypass in existing Agent Trace Lab**. No actual tool executed and no user data supplied.
- DELIVERED RESEARCH: Created original source-based [docs/INSPECT_APPROVAL_EXECUTION_GAP_2026-11-21.md](INSPECT_APPROVAL_EXECUTION_GAP_2026-11-21.md) documenting exact schema terms, native/bridge identity ambiguity, experimental field risk, consent-vs-operator-approval distinction, privacy defaults, counterexample and safe design. Agent Trace Lab runtime code unchanged, no adapter created; do not manufacture grants or execution from approval records. NO GO for an automatic Inspect authorization bridge or security release without independently sourced user consent, actual execution evidence and pinned versioned schema.
- NEXT_ACTION: ATL-004BO (bounded 15–25 minute evidence-driven portfolio feature selection): given proven Inspect linkage/consent blocker, STOP adapter implementation until authorized actual native approval+execution records are available. Evaluate two maintained open-source gaps with safe, attributable public issues and direct acceptance tests, prioritizing an existing idle owned AI/IT repo. Confirm correct KIRAA/Core, exact Git revision/dirty state/ownership; select a genuinely reproducible user-facing feature, not more small speculative defensive edits. Implement one ≤30-minute task only if an actual failing case is demonstrated and a clean isolated checkout exists. Otherwise document rigorous blocker and NEXT_ACTION without creating a repository or falsifying release quality. Preserve PiP running app and KPC/KiraShop/Motion/Klipboard/Debug Bridge sessions.
- NEXT_ACTION_ACCEPTANCE: grounded source comparison (with originality and feasibility, >=85/100 admission for a NEW public repo), exact red/green test and CI/PR receipts for any authorized source changes, otherwise document the concrete blocker; clean synchronized Git and durable campaign checkpoint. Research-only Agent Trace Lab release gates remain HOLD.

This reproduces the direct-write failure mechanism, NOT a claimed observed real PiP settings loss or actual operating-system crash. All probe files were removed safely.
- IMPLEMENTATION: In existing `src/engine.rs`, serialize first, `create_new` a unique private staging file **in the same target folder**, write full JSON, `sync_all`, close, then use `fs::rename` to replace `settings.json`. A failed stage or publish retains the previous complete destination and attempts to remove the stage. Unique PID/timestamp/atomic-counter naming reduces collisions without deleting older files; failed rename may occur on locked Windows files. Added two deterministic Rust unit tests in isolated temporary folders: full replacement/valid JSON/no leftover stage and injected publish failure leaving original byte-identical and stage removed. Updated README explaining failure limits, no new dependencies, no recovery-journal changes. Only `src/engine.rs` and README edited. ### ATL-004BU original two-process checkout contention and worktree isolation — 2026-11-21

- ROUTE/RECOVER: KIRAA through KPC Core, existing campaign main 7c3da48c72bb866edc6c756948c9f69235c1c627 clean/synced with exact [docs CI 44864006424](https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/actions/runs/44864006424) SUCCESS; earlier [source PR #61](https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/pull/61) merged at 40261777d0137d1c5de5118a2d95ef2541a8cd0c, [merged-main CI 44863773620](https://github.com/AkiyamaKira2003/kira-agent-trace-lab/actions/runs/44863773620) SUCCESS. No project.state/source=loaded KPC governance verified. No source edits in this task.
- ORIGINAL SYNTHETIC COLLISION TEST (NOT CUSTOMER TELEMETRY): Created a uniquely named, validated temporary root immediately under C:\Projects, initialized one new empty Git repository and committed self-authored shared.txt with baseline text. Two separate Python interpreter processes each independently read the same baseline, signaled readiness through marker files OUTSIDE the Git repo, blocked on a release marker, and then concurrently overwrote the SAME checkout file. Actual observed: both read baseline; final shared checkout held only **B|saw=baseline**, so the A write did not survive. This demonstrates a deliberately constructed lost-update race on one file, not an observed collision in live KPC or user projects.
- ISOLATED COMPARISON: From the same baseline Git HEAD, created two separate branch worktrees under that same temporary root (actor-a and actor-b), ran the same two independent processes with the same readiness barrier but with separate destination paths. Observed BOTH outputs retained: actor-a had **A|saw=baseline**, actor-b had **B|saw=baseline**; git worktree list reported 3 total worktrees (main and 2 branches). This confirms directory isolation for this toy write race, NOT a general concurrency/recovery guarantee or a proof that ordinary Git worktrees provide a lease.
- LIMITATION REPRO: Before unblocking the two live Python processes on the shared checkout (while Git HEAD/working files were still clean), the actual recently merged scripts/workspace_preflight.py returned **no_observed_blocker**, with zero executable paths rooted in the checkout. Both actors were nevertheless active as interpreter processes whose executable image lived OUTSIDE the repository. This is an experimentally confirmed coverage gap of the documented snapshot heuristic, not a newly introduced regression: the helper explicitly warns that it CANNOT detect interpreted agent sessions, other working-directory owners, locks or TOCTOU races. Do not use exit 0 as approval for destructive work or market it as safe multi-agent coordination.
- HARNESS CONTROL/SAFETY: First ephemeral harness attempt failed (worker argument-dispatch typo / readiness timeout); inspected the failure and verified its own temp root had been removed and no matching owned Python processes remained. Corrected dispatch in a second separate temporary harness, added cleanup of only its own spawned child processes, and reran successfully. After pass, verified **zero remaining ATL004BU-TEMP directories and zero Python experiment processes**; campaign main remained clean/synced. Only self-authored temp files and temp branches were created/removed. No KPC/KiraShop/PiP/Motion/Klipboard/Debug Bridge checkout touched, no process killed except its own temporary children on hypothetical failure.
- FEATURE/PRODUCT DECISION: The real underlying worktree/shared-file risk is corroborated by [Claude Code #88862](https://github.com/anthropics/claude-code/issues/88862) and [Hermes Agent #46303](https://github.com/NousResearch/hermes-agent/issues/46303), but [beyond10x/worktree](https://github.com/beyond10x/worktree) already offers mature leases/recovery and this measured toy collision does not prove a novel prevention algorithm. The prior subjective Windows multi-agent new-project score **79/100** remains below the >=85/100 admission gate. **NO GO** for turning the small read-only preflight into a purported lease manager, releasing a new repo or claiming real-world safety. This task delivered independent experiment evidence and a clear blocker; **no PR/source changes or CI rerun** were warranted for this docs-only experiment.
- NEXT_ACTION: ATL-004BV (bounded 15–25 minute DISTINCT USER-VALUE INVESTIGATION): stop reworking preflight, generic worktree managers, OTLP, Sudoku and signed updater tests. Search 2-3 independently evidenced higher-value AI/IT needs in existing idle owned repos or permitted upstream contribution surfaces with real issue demand, demoable AI/cross-language engineering and rights. Require a genuinely differentiated runnable acceptance scenario before any new >=85/100 public flagship; otherwise report a source-grounded blocker.
